*   The paper makes a substantial contribution by introducing a novel benchmark that addresses key limitations in the evaluation of agentic tool use. The shift to real-world tools, the expansive action space, and the outcome-based evaluation are all significant improvements.
*   The benchmark results provide valuable insights into the capabilities and limitations of current LLMs, highlighting areas for future research.
*   The paper is well-written and clearly articulates its contributions.
*   However, the weaknesses described above (limited model scope, potential for task creation bias, reliance on proprietary evaluation models) prevent it from achieving a higher score. While acknowledging the value of real-world tools, the ethical considerations of the tool usage are missing.
*   The lack of a retriever and the decision to deliberately use a "simple evaluation pipeline" leave important questions about what can improve and how tools can be incorporated into solving a problem.

The paper demonstrates strong novelty and significance, but there are avenues for further improvement. It sets a strong foundation for future research.
Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### On the Evolution of Federated Post-Training Large Language Models: A Model Accessibility View
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16261v1)
- **Authors**: Tao Guo, Junxiao Wang, Fushuo Huo, Laizhong Cui, Song Guo, Jie Gui, Dacheng Tao
- **Abstract**: Federated Learning (FL) enables training models across decentralized data silos while preserving client data privacy. Recent research has explored efficient methods for post-training large language models (LLMs) within FL to address computational and communication challenges. While existing approaches often rely on access to LLMs' internal information, which is frequently restricted in real-world scenarios, an inference-only paradigm (black-box FedLLM) has emerged to address these limitations. This paper presents a comprehensive survey on federated tuning for LLMs. We propose a taxonomy categorizing existing studies along two axes: model access-based and parameter efficiency-based optimization. We classify FedLLM approaches into white-box, gray-box, and black-box techniques, highlighting representative methods within each category. We review emerging research treating LLMs as black-box inference APIs and discuss promising directions and open challenges for future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive survey of Federated Learning (FL) techniques applied to Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically focusing on federated post-training. It introduces a novel taxonomy for FedLLM approaches, categorizing them based on two axes: model access (white-box, gray-box, black-box) and parameter efficiency (full model update, partial parameter update, input-level update).  The survey examines existing research in each category, with particular attention given to emerging approaches that treat LLMs as black-box inference APIs.  The paper also discusses open challenges and future research directions, highlighting the need for improved federated value alignment in inference-only and black-box settings, along with enhanced security and privacy measures.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Scope:** The paper covers a wide range of FedLLM techniques, providing a valuable overview of the current landscape.
*   **Novel Taxonomy:**  The proposed taxonomy based on model accessibility and parameter efficiency is a significant contribution. It provides a structured framework for understanding and comparing different FedLLM approaches, addressing a gap in the existing literature. This is a clear and insightful way to categorize existing research.
*   **Focus on Black-Box FedLLM:** The emphasis on black-box FedLLM and inference-only APIs is timely and relevant. As LLMs become increasingly proprietary, this area will become more important, and the survey addresses the key challenges and opportunities.
*   **Discussion of Future Directions:** The discussion of federated value alignment (DPO, RLHF, RLAIF) and security/privacy concerns in black-box settings is valuable and identifies important areas for future research.
*   **Well-Organized and Clearly Written:** The paper is well-structured, easy to follow, and the concepts are clearly explained.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Depth of Technical Detail:** Given the breadth of the survey, the technical details of each approach are somewhat limited. The paper provides a high-level overview but might lack sufficient detail for researchers looking for implementation specifics.
*   **Lack of Comparative Benchmarking:** While the survey categorizes different approaches, it doesn't offer a comparative analysis of their performance in various settings. A table comparing accuracy, communication cost, and privacy guarantees across different methods would add significant value.
*   **Somewhat Optimistic Tone:**  The paper presents a rather optimistic view of the existing methods and future directions. It could benefit from a more critical assessment of the limitations and challenges that remain, even within the promising research areas identified.
*   **The categorization may not be entirely mutually exclusive**. Some methods may fall in between categories or have aspects of different boxes.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The primary novelty lies in the proposed taxonomy and the explicit focus on black-box FedLLM and inference-only APIs.  While other surveys exist, this paper provides a unique perspective and framework that is highly relevant given the current trends in LLM development.  The paper is significant because it addresses the growing importance of federated learning in the context of increasingly inaccessible LLMs, highlighting the need for research that can overcome these limitations. The thorough organization and clear taxonomy make this an excellent resource for researchers and practitioners entering the field.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **8**.  The paper provides a valuable service to the community by synthesizing and organizing the growing body of research on FedLLM. The novel taxonomy and the focus on black-box approaches are significant contributions. However, the lack of comparative benchmarking and slightly optimistic tone prevent it from achieving a higher score.  It serves as an excellent starting point for researchers in the field and provides a solid framework for future work. The thorough literature review provides a high-level overview of the field to better understand it. Future works can now leverage this taxonomy.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLMs that Understand Processes: Instruction-tuning for Semantics-Aware Process Mining
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16270v1)
- **Authors**: Vira Pyrih, Adrian Rebmann, Han van der Aa
- **Abstract**: Process mining is increasingly using textual information associated with events to tackle tasks such as anomaly detection and process discovery. Such semantics-aware process mining focuses on what behavior should be possible in a process (i.e., expectations), thus providing an important complement to traditional, frequency-based techniques that focus on recorded behavior (i.e., reality). Large Language Models (LLMs) provide a powerful means for tackling semantics-aware tasks. However, the best performance is so far achieved through task-specific fine-tuning, which is computationally intensive and results in models that can only handle one specific task. To overcome this lack of generalization, we use this paper to investigate the potential of instruction-tuning for semantics-aware process mining. The idea of instruction-tuning here is to expose an LLM to prompt-answer pairs for different tasks, e.g., anomaly detection and next-activity prediction, making it more familiar with process mining, thus allowing it to also perform better at unseen tasks, such as process discovery. Our findings demonstrate a varied impact of instruction-tuning: while performance considerably improved on process discovery and prediction tasks, it varies across models on anomaly detection tasks, highlighting that the selection of tasks for instruction-tuning is critical to achieving desired outcomes.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of instruction-tuning for large language models (LLMs) in semantics-aware process mining. Instruction-tuning involves fine-tuning LLMs with prompt-answer pairs covering different process mining tasks (anomaly detection, next-activity prediction, process discovery). The goal is to improve the LLMs' ability to generalize across various tasks and prompts not seen during the fine-tuning phase. The study evaluates the performance of instruction-tuned LLMs against untuned and task-specific fine-tuned models on several control-flow-oriented process mining tasks. The findings suggest that instruction-tuning enhances generalization, especially for discovery and predictive tasks, while challenges remain for anomaly detection. The work demonstrates that a single instruction-tuned LLM can perform multiple semantics-aware process mining tasks, providing a more scalable approach compared to single-task fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel application of instruction-tuning to semantics-aware process mining. While instruction-tuning itself is not new, its systematic exploration within this domain constitutes a significant contribution. Previous research has primarily focused on task-specific fine-tuning.  The explicit aim of improving *generalization* across different process mining tasks, rather than solely optimizing for individual tasks, is a key differentiator.
* **Significance:** The findings have potential implications for the process mining community. The paper demonstrates that instruction-tuning offers a path toward more scalable and flexible process analysis tools. Single model can perform several tasks instead of needing specific fine-tuned model per task. However, the mixed results (especially regarding anomaly detection), indicate the need for further investigation to overcome the challanges. The findings suggest that task selection is crucial for achieving the desired outcomes in instruction-tuning.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Systematic Evaluation:** The paper offers a systematic evaluation of instruction-tuning across various process mining tasks. The leave-one-group-out evaluation setup ensures a fair comparison between different models and tasks.
    * **Well-Defined Methodology:** The authors provide a clear description of the methodology, including the instruction-tuning procedure, training data sampling, and performance measures.
    * **Dataset Creation:** The creation and public release of a dedicated process mining instruction dataset is a valuable contribution to the research community.
    * **Qualitative Analysis:** The qualitative analysis provides insights into the models' understanding of process behavior, going beyond aggregate performance metrics.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope:** The study focuses on control-flow oriented tasks and structured inputs, which may not fully capture the complexity of real-world event logs.
    * **Inconsistent Results:** The mixed results on anomaly detection tasks raise questions about the limitations of instruction-tuning in certain scenarios.  The performance degradation in Llama IT for anomaly detection tasks highlights potential risks of instruction-tuning if not done carefully.
    * **Benchmarking against task-specific fine-tuning:** While the results were compared against task-specific fine tuning models in previous work, a direct benchmarking of instruction-tuning vs. task-specific fine-tuning, is missing.
* **Potential Influence:** This paper is a first step in a promising direction. By highlighting the benefits and challenges of instruction-tuning in process mining, it motivates further research on this topic. The paper opens up the door to new research directions, such as exploring different instruction-tuning strategies, incorporating other process perspectives, and developing more robust evaluation benchmarks for real-world event logs.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a novel contribution by applying instruction-tuning to semantics-aware process mining and demonstrating its potential for improving generalization across tasks. The systematic evaluation, methodology, and dataset creation are notable strengths. However, the limited scope and inconsistent results on anomaly detection tasks indicate that further research is needed. Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, the paper is a valuable contribution to the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Structuring GUI Elements through Vision Language Models: Towards Action Space Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16271v1)
- **Authors**: Yi Xu, Yesheng Zhang, jiajia Liu, Jingdong Chen
- **Abstract**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have emerged as pivotal tools in enhancing human-computer interaction. In this paper we focus on the application of MLLMs in the field of graphical user interface (GUI) elements structuring, where they assist in processing user instructions based on screen contents. Despite the promise of MLLMs, their performance in precisely generating UI element coordinates, a critical aspect of GUI understanding, is hindered by the nature of next-token prediction training. This challenge arises from the semantic void surrounding numerical UI coordinates in language representation spaces, necessitating a substantial and diverse dataset to bolster visual module capabilities. To address these limitations, we introduce an IoU-Augmented Maximum Likelihood (IAML) training paradigm. Specifically, our approach involves a novel pipeline for IoU-based coordinate sampling to augment the training data, which considers the proximity to ground truth coordinates. This data augmentation strategy is then employed to fine-tune MLLMs under the IAML paradigm, which is designed to mitigate the exposure bias problem inherent in traditional maximum likelihood estimation. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate the superior performance of our IAML training approach over traditional training paradigms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper addresses the challenge of training Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to accurately structure Graphical User Interface (GUI) elements, particularly in generating precise UI element coordinates. The authors argue that standard next-token prediction training is insufficient due to the semantic gap surrounding numerical UI coordinates. To overcome this, they introduce an IoU-Augmented Maximum Likelihood (IAML) training paradigm. IAML involves an IoU-based coordinate sampling pipeline for data augmentation, which prioritizes coordinates spatially close to the ground truth. This augmented data is then used to fine-tune the MLLM, mitigating the exposure bias inherent in traditional Maximum Likelihood Estimation (MLE). Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of IAML compared to standard training paradigms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the IAML training paradigm, specifically the IoU-based data augmentation strategy. Augmenting training data based on the proximity (measured by IoU) to ground truth coordinates is a sensible and potentially effective approach for improving coordinate prediction in MLLMs.  However, the idea of using IoU as a metric for reward/loss augmentation is not entirely novel; it has precedents in object detection tasks. The authors need to clearly differentiate their specific implementation and demonstrate why it is particularly suited for the GUI element structuring problem compared to other existing IoU-based data augmentation techniques. The contribution boils down to adapting an existing idea to a specific problem setting (GUI structure prediction), which reduces the overall novelty.

* **Significance:** Accurate GUI element structuring is crucial for enabling MLLMs to effectively interact with computer interfaces and execute user instructions. Improving the precision of coordinate generation directly impacts the usability of MLLM-based GUI agents. While the paper shows performance gains, the magnitude of improvement, especially when considering the full dataset setting, appears relatively modest (around 1-2% increase in F1 score). The low-resource setting shows a more significant relative improvement, which is a strong point.  However, the overall practical impact might be limited if the improvement doesn't translate to significant real-world usability gains.  Furthermore, the paper focuses primarily on *accuracy*. Other important aspects, such as generalization to unseen UIs, robustness to noisy inputs, and computational efficiency, are not thoroughly addressed.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of standard training approaches for precise coordinate generation.
    * **Intuitive Approach:**  The IoU-based data augmentation is a conceptually straightforward and intuitive way to address the semantic gap surrounding UI coordinates.
    * **Demonstrated Improvement:**  Experiments show consistent improvements over baseline methods.
    * **Low-Resource Performance:** The better performance in the low-resource scenario suggests potential for broader applicability.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Novelty:**  The underlying idea of IoU-based data augmentation has precedents in other areas. The authors could have more explicitly highlighted the uniqueness of their approach, like, how it differs from existing techniques and why this difference matters for GUI elements structuring.
    * **Modest Improvement:** The absolute performance gains in the full dataset setting are relatively small.
    * **Limited Scope:**  The paper focuses mainly on accuracy and does not comprehensively evaluate other important aspects of GUI agent performance (e.g., generalization, robustness, efficiency).
    * **Lack of Ablation Study**: The paper would be strengthened by an ablation study breaking down the contribution of each component of the approach.
    * **Lack of Comparison**: A missing part of the paper is the direct comparison to the SOTA UI structuring approach in the same setup.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence the development of more accurate and usable MLLM-based GUI agents. However, the impact will likely be incremental unless the approach can be scaled up or adapted to address other challenges in GUI understanding. The method could also be a valuable augmentation technique in conjunction with other advances in MLLM architectures and training methods.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, a score of 6 seems appropriate. While the paper presents a sensible and effective approach, the novelty is incremental, and the performance gains are modest in some scenarios. The lack of a clear comparison to SOTA and the limited evaluation scope also detract from the overall impact.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### AgentScope 1.0: A Developer-Centric Framework for Building Agentic Applications
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16279v1)
- **Authors**: Dawei Gao, Zitao Li, Yuexiang Xie, Weirui Kuang, Liuyi Yao, Bingchen Qian, Zhijian Ma, Yue Cui, Haohao Luo, Shen Li, Lu Yi, Yi Yu, Shiqi He, Zhiling Luo, Wenmeng Zhou, Zhicheng Zhang, Xuguang He, Ziqian Chen, Weikai Liao, Farruh Isakulovich Kushnazarov, Yaliang Li, Bolin Ding, Jingren Zhou
- **Abstract**: Driven by rapid advancements of Large Language Models (LLMs), agents are empowered to combine intrinsic knowledge with dynamic tool use, greatly enhancing their capacity to address real-world tasks. In line with such an evolution, AgentScope introduces major improvements in a new version (1.0), towards comprehensively supporting flexible and efficient tool-based agent-environment interactions for building agentic applications. Specifically, we abstract foundational components essential for agentic applications and provide unified interfaces and extensible modules, enabling developers to easily leverage the latest progress, such as new models and MCPs. Furthermore, we ground agent behaviors in the ReAct paradigm and offer advanced agent-level infrastructure based on a systematic asynchronous design, which enriches both human-agent and agent-agent interaction patterns while improving execution efficiency. Building on this foundation, we integrate several built-in agents tailored to specific practical scenarios. AgentScope also includes robust engineering support for developer-friendly experiences. We provide a scalable evaluation module with a visual studio interface, making the development of long-trajectory agentic applications more manageable and easier to trace. In addition, AgentScope offers a runtime sandbox to ensure safe agent execution and facilitates rapid deployment in production environments. With these enhancements, AgentScope provides a practical foundation for building scalable, adaptive, and effective agentic applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the AgentScope 1.0 paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AgentScope 1.0, a developer-centric framework designed to facilitate the creation of agentic applications utilizing Large Language Models (LLMs). AgentScope aims to streamline the development process by abstracting foundational components, providing unified interfaces, and supporting flexible tool-based agent-environment interactions, specifically leveraging the ReAct paradigm. Key features include foundational components (message, model, memory, tool modules), agent-level infrastructure with built-in agents, asynchronous execution, real-time steering, a developer-friendly evaluation module, Studio for visual debugging, and a runtime sandbox for safe deployment. The framework supports diverse LLM providers, multimodal inputs, and incorporates mechanisms for state persistence and non-invasive customization. The paper presents various usage examples demonstrating AgentScope's capabilities in user-assistant conversations, multi-agent interactions, deep research, and browser-based tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper claims to introduce a *new* version of AgentScope but often compares the new version to what are presented as gaps in *existing* LLM-based agent frameworks (i.e. reliance on intrinsic reasoning, support for tool-based perception). The paper positions itself as a practical framework grounded in ReAct, offering significant improvements over existing solutions by comprehensively supporting flexible and efficient tool-based agent-environment interactions.  However, many agent frameworks already implement ReAct, tool usage, and abstracted components. The novelty appears primarily in the specific combination of features, developer tooling, and architectural choices within AgentScope. The dual-client architecture for MCP management and the group-wise tool management strategy introduce somewhat novel aspects.
*   **Significance:** The significance hinges on the framework's ability to truly simplify agent development and deployment in practical, real-world scenarios.  The focus on asynchronous execution, real-time steering, and the robust engineering toolkits suggests a strong emphasis on production-readiness, which is valuable. If the framework gains adoption, the standardized interfaces and tools could contribute to greater interoperability and collaboration in the agentic application space. However, there are now many agent frameworks, so the significance depends on AgentScope carving out a unique niche, demonstrating superior performance, or fostering a strong community. The integration with OpenTelemetry and various existing services also suggests a design geared toward practical application.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Feature Set:** AgentScope integrates a wide array of features, addressing various aspects of agent development from foundational components to deployment considerations.
    *   **Developer-Friendly Tooling:** The inclusion of the evaluation module, Studio, and runtime sandbox demonstrates a strong commitment to developer experience, which can significantly reduce the barrier to entry.
    *   **Production-Focused Design:** The emphasis on asynchronous execution, state persistence, and secure tool execution positions AgentScope as a viable option for building production-grade agentic applications.
    *   **Flexibility and Extensibility:** The modular design and support for diverse LLM providers allow developers to adapt and customize the framework to their specific needs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Empirical Evaluation:** The paper would benefit from more quantitative evaluations demonstrating the performance improvements of AgentScope over other frameworks in specific tasks or benchmarks. This is a notable omission.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** As mentioned previously, the individual components are not entirely novel; the primary contribution lies in the specific combination and integration.
    *   **Potential for Over-Engineering:** The extensive feature set could introduce unnecessary complexity for simpler use cases.  The paper could benefit from highlighting clear use cases and guiding developers towards the most relevant components.
    *   **Dependency on Alibaba Infrastructure:** The association with Alibaba Group might raise concerns about vendor lock-in or biases in the framework's design.
    *   **Assumed Familiarity:** The paper assumes a certain level of familiarity with agentic paradigms, LLMs, and related technologies, making it potentially inaccessible to newcomers.

*   **Potential Influence:** The combination of features and focus on developer experience has the potential to influence how agentic applications are built, especially if AgentScope demonstrates superior performance or ease of use compared to existing solutions.  The success hinges on fostering a strong community and attracting developers to adopt the framework.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the combination of the strengths and weaknesses, a score of **6** is appropriate. While AgentScope provides a comprehensive and production-oriented feature set, its novelty is somewhat incremental, and the paper lacks strong empirical validation. The focus on developer tooling is promising, but the framework's success will depend on adoption and demonstrated advantages over existing alternatives. The concerns regarding potential over-engineering and dependency on a specific company also limit the score.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### A Sharp KL-Convergence Analysis for Diffusion Models under Minimal Assumptions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16306v1)
- **Authors**: Nishant Jain, Tong Zhang
- **Abstract**: Diffusion-based generative models have emerged as highly effective methods for synthesizing high-quality samples. Recent works have focused on analyzing the convergence of their generation process with minimal assumptions, either through reverse SDEs or Probability Flow ODEs. The best known guarantees, without any smoothness assumptions, for the KL divergence so far achieve a linear dependence on the data dimension $d$ and an inverse quadratic dependence on $\varepsilon$. In this work, we present a refined analysis that improves the dependence on $\varepsilon$. We model the generation process as a composition of two steps: a reverse ODE step, followed by a smaller noising step along the forward process. This design leverages the fact that the ODE step enables control in Wasserstein-type error, which can then be converted into a KL divergence bound via noise addition, leading to a better dependence on the discretization step size. We further provide a novel analysis to achieve the linear $d$-dependence for the error due to discretizing this Probability Flow ODE in absence of any smoothness assumptions. We show that $\tilde{O}\left(\tfrac{d\log^{3/2}(\frac{1}{\delta})}{\varepsilon}\right)$ steps suffice to approximate the target distribution corrupted with Gaussian noise of variance $\delta$ within $O(\varepsilon^2)$ in KL divergence, improving upon the previous best result, requiring $\tilde{O}\left(\tfrac{d\log^2(\frac{1}{\delta})}{\varepsilon^2}\right)$ steps.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and evaluation of the paper "A Sharp KL-Convergence Analysis for Diffusion Models under Minimal Assumptions":

**Summary:**

The paper presents a refined theoretical analysis of the convergence of diffusion models.  It focuses on improving the dependence on the discretization step size (ε) for KL-divergence between the generated distribution and the target distribution (perturbed by Gaussian noise).  The authors model the generative process as a two-step procedure: a reverse ODE step followed by a smaller noising step (forward SDE).  This approach leverages the fact that the ODE step offers better control over Wasserstein-type error, which is then converted to a KL-divergence bound by adding noise. They also provide a novel analysis to achieve a linear dependence on the data dimension *d* for the discretization error of the Probability Flow ODE without smoothness assumptions.  The paper claims that O˜(d/ε) steps suffice to approximate the target distribution corrupted with Gaussian noise of variance δ within O(ε²) in KL divergence, improving upon the previous best results which required O˜(d/ε²) steps.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the two-step approach (reverse ODE + forward noise) and its accompanying analysis to improve the ε dependence of the KL-divergence convergence. While others have explored similar themes of minimizing assumptions and improving convergence rates in diffusion models, this specific combination and the claimed sharpness of the bound are contributions. The novel derivation of the d-dependence through clever manipulations involving relations between the score function and its higher order derivatives during the ODE path also contributes. The paper correctly cites relevant baselines for the analysis of reverse SDEs using stochastic localization.

**Significance:** Diffusion models have become incredibly popular, so understanding their theoretical properties, particularly the convergence rate, is important. Reducing the required number of steps for convergence (improving on the best-known guarantees) has practical implications, as it directly relates to the efficiency of sample generation. The removal of strict smoothness assumptions also makes the analysis more relevant to real-world scenarios where data and learned score functions may not be perfectly smooth. The analysis has a subtle improvement using Wasserstein bounds as intermediate bounds. The practical impact of moving from ε² to ε might, however, be minor, depending on the constants involved. The TV to KL relationship is relevant to note as well.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies the gap in the literature – the suboptimal ε dependence in KL divergence guarantees for diffusion models.
*   **Technically Sound:** The paper appears to provide a mathematically rigorous analysis, with detailed proofs provided in the appendix (although a full audit would require deep expertise in the field).
*   **Relevant Results:** The improved convergence rate is a valuable theoretical contribution to the understanding of diffusion models.
*   **Well-Referenced:** The paper provides an adequate overview of related work and properly acknowledges the contributions of other researchers.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Practical Impact:** The improvement in the theoretical convergence rate may not translate directly into a significant practical speedup in sample generation, as other factors (e.g., the complexity of score function estimation) can dominate the computational cost. The o˜ notation also hides potentially large constants, masking the actual gains. More detailed evaluation should address if this improvement in convergence is practically implementable and how it is compared to previous works.
*   **Complexity of Analysis:** As the authors themselves state, the ODE path analysis involving higher order gradient terms makes analysis more complex. This may limit its accessibility.

**Justification for Score:**

This paper presents a solid theoretical improvement on the convergence analysis of diffusion models. The two-step approach and the refined analysis leading to improved error dependence are novel. While the real-world impact of the faster theoretical convergence and the constants hidden by soft-O is not obvious, the theoretical contribution to the deep learning community is valuable.

Score: 6/10

- **Classification**: stat.ML
- **Score**: 6/10

### Exploiting Information Redundancy in Attention Maps for Extreme Quantization of Vision Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16311v1)
- **Authors**: Lucas Maisonnave, Karim Haroun, Tom Pegeot
- **Abstract**: Transformer models rely on Multi-Head Self-Attention (MHSA) mechanisms, where each attention head contributes to the final representation. However, their computational complexity and high memory demands due to MHSA hinders their deployment at the edge. In this work, we analyze and exploit information redundancy in attention maps to accelerate model inference. By quantifying the information captured by each attention head using Shannon entropy, our analysis reveals that attention heads with lower entropy, i.e., exhibiting more deterministic behavior, tend to contribute less information, motivating targeted compression strategies. Relying on these insights, we propose Entropy Attention Maps (EAM), a model that freezes the weights of low-entropy attention maps and quantizes these values to low precision to avoid redundant re-computation. Empirical validation on ImageNet-1k shows that EAM achieves similar or higher accuracy at $\leq$20\% sparsity in attention maps and competitive performance beyond this level for the DeiT and Swin Transformer models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Exploiting Information Redundancy in Attention Maps for Extreme Quantization of Vision Transformers":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the computational and memory demands of Vision Transformers (ViTs) by exploring redundancy in attention maps. The core idea is that not all attention heads contribute equally to the model's performance. The authors propose a method called Entropy Attention Maps (EAM) that uses Shannon entropy to quantify the information captured by each attention head. Low-entropy heads, indicating more deterministic behavior, are considered less informative and are therefore targeted for compression via freezing their weights and quantizing them to lower precision (4 bits).  The authors demonstrate through experiments on ImageNet-1K with DeiT and Swin Transformer models that EAM can achieve similar or even improved accuracy compared to a baseline quantized model (RepQ-ViT) at sparsity levels below 20%, and competitive performance even at higher levels. They also show that their method outperforms random fixing of weights.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Interesting Insight:** The paper offers a valuable perspective on attention heads, showing that they have variable information content and demonstrating that entropy is a useful metric to quantify this. This challenges the assumption that all heads need to be treated equally in compression strategies.
*   **Quantifiable Metric:** The use of Shannon entropy provides a principled and quantifiable approach to identify redundant attention heads, moving beyond ad-hoc pruning methods.
*   **Targeted Compression Strategy:**  The paper proposes a specific and practical compression strategy – freezing and quantizing low-entropy heads –  that directly leverages the insight about information redundancy.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments are reasonably thorough, covering different ViT architectures (DeiT, Swin) and showing competitive performance against a strong quantization baseline (RepQ-ViT).  The ablation study with random fixing provides evidence for the effectiveness of the entropy-based selection of heads to compress.
*   **Potential for Hardware Acceleration:** Fixing and quantizing parts of the attention layers has a potential to reduce complexity and allow for dedicated hardware acceleration.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the idea of using entropy to guide compression is interesting, applying quantization techniques to transformers has been widely researched. The contribution lies primarily in targeting attention heads specifically based on entropy, and in the experimental validation of this idea.
*   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** While the experimental results are promising, the paper lacks a deeper theoretical explanation as to *why* low-entropy heads are less important and what specific properties of attention maps cause the difference in entropy. This might limit the broader applicability and generalizability of the approach.
*   **Limited Comparison with other Compression Techniques** While they present an ablation study on random fixing, the related work is lacking a comparison with the more advanced and state-of-the-art methods like Token Merging and Pruning.
*   **Specific Architecture Tuning and Scalability:** The specific sparsity levels achieved and the benefits derived from them might be highly dependent on the ViT architecture and dataset. It's unclear how these findings would translate to significantly larger models or more complex datasets without further tuning.
*   **Minor performance gains:** The paper mentions that fixing and quantizing attention weights can lead to enhanced performance. However, it is worth noting that the gain is often modest and needs more justification.

**Significance and Potential Influence:**

The paper could have a moderate impact on the field of ViT compression. It presents a practical and potentially effective method for reducing the computational and memory footprint of these models. The use of entropy as a guiding metric for compression offers a novel direction that could be further explored. The results could inspire further research into more sophisticated methods for analyzing and exploiting redundancy in attention mechanisms.
However, it is worth noting that the performance gains are minor and that the current state of the art research focuses on other methods of compression that often leads to larger performance gains.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper contributes a valuable insight regarding the information content of attention heads, quantified through entropy, and demonstrates its utility for targeted compression. However, the overall novelty is somewhat incremental given the existing body of work on transformer quantization. The lack of a deeper theoretical analysis and a more comprehensive comparison with other relevant compression techniques are weaknesses. While the empirical results are convincing, the potential for significant performance improvements and broader applicability remains to be fully demonstrated.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Retrieval Enhanced Feedback via In-context Neural Error-book
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16313v1)
- **Authors**: Jongyeop Hyun, Bumsoo Kim
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have significantly improved reasoning capabilities, with in-context learning (ICL) emerging as a key technique for adaptation without retraining. While previous works have focused on leveraging correct examples, recent research highlights the importance of learning from errors to enhance performance. However, existing methods lack a structured framework for analyzing and mitigating errors, particularly in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs), where integrating visual and textual inputs adds complexity. To address this issue, we propose REFINE: Retrieval-Enhanced Feedback via In-context Neural Error-book, a teacher-student framework that systematically structures errors and provides targeted feedback. REFINE introduces three systematic queries to construct structured feedback -- Feed-Target, Feed-Check, and Feed-Path -- to enhance multimodal reasoning by prioritizing relevant visual information, diagnosing critical failure points, and formulating corrective actions. Unlike prior approaches that rely on redundant retrievals, REFINE optimizes structured feedback retrieval, improving inference efficiency, token usage, and scalability. Our results demonstrate substantial speedup, reduced computational costs, and successful generalization, highlighting REFINE's potential for enhancing multimodal reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces REFINE: Retrieval-Enhanced Feedback via In-context Neural Error-book, a teacher-student framework for improving multimodal reasoning in Large Language Models (MLLMs). REFINE structures errors and provides targeted feedback using three systematic queries: Feed-Target, Feed-Check, and Feed-Path. This approach aims to enhance multimodal reasoning by prioritizing relevant visual information, diagnosing failure points, and formulating corrective actions.  Unlike previous approaches that rely on redundant retrievals, REFINE optimizes structured feedback retrieval to improve inference efficiency, reduce computational costs, and enhance scalability. The paper presents empirical results demonstrating substantial speedup, reduced token usage, and successful generalization compared to baseline methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the structured approach to error analysis and feedback generation specifically tailored for MLLMs. While error-driven learning and in-context learning have been explored previously, the application of a structured framework (inspired by educational psychology principles) to multimodal reasoning is a distinctive contribution. The systematic feedback queries (Feed-Target, Feed-Check, Feed-Path) and their integration into a teacher-student framework provide a clear and practical method for error mitigation in MLLMs. The deterministic single-nearest-neighbor retrieval is also a noteworthy departure from stochastic approaches.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in MLLMs: the lack of a structured approach for analyzing and mitigating errors, particularly when integrating visual and textual inputs. The demonstrated improvements in inference efficiency, token usage, and generalization are significant, potentially enabling more efficient and scalable deployment of MLLMs in real-world applications. The successful generalization from smaller to larger datasets suggests the practical applicability of the approach.  The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component, strengthening the results' robustness.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-defined framework for error analysis and feedback generation.
    *   Empirical results demonstrating substantial improvements in efficiency and generalization.
    *   Detailed ablation studies analyzing the contribution of each feedback component.
    *   Practical approach readily applicable to existing MLLMs.
    *   Addresses a known challenge of integrating visual and textual data in MLLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on the quality of teacher-generated feedback could be a limitation. The paper mentions using Gemini models for feedback generation, which may introduce biases or inaccuracies.
    *   The framework's effectiveness may be limited to errors needing complex knowledge synthesis or when generalizing to entirely new task domains.
    *   The current approach seems to assume that errors are primarily due to incorrect procedural knowledge or execution, rather than fundamental knowledge gaps or biases in the MLLM's training data.
    *   The experimental evaluation focuses on three multimodal benchmarks. It needs to be confirmed on a wider range of tasks.

*   **Potential Impact:** REFINE has the potential to influence future research on error mitigation and in-context learning for MLLMs. It could inspire the development of more structured and targeted feedback mechanisms for improving the reliability and interpretability of MLLMs in various applications. The teacher-student framework could be extended to incorporate human-in-the-loop feedback or reinforcement learning techniques for further improvement.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:** The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by providing a structured framework for error analysis and feedback generation in MLLMs. The empirical results demonstrate significant improvements in efficiency and generalization. The proposed framework has a well-defined structure and empirical support, it is significantly novel and will prove helpful in the field. However, the reliance on teacher-generated feedback and limitations in addressing complex knowledge gaps slightly reduce the score. Its novelty is above average, and the impact will be high, but needs to be confirmed with further experiments and wider application.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### OwkinZero: Accelerating Biological Discovery with AI
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16315v1)
- **Authors**: Nathan Bigaud, Vincent Cabeli, Meltem Gurel, Arthur Pignet, John Klein, Gilles Wainrib, Eric Durand
- **Abstract**: While large language models (LLMs) are rapidly advancing scientific research, they continue to struggle with core biological reasoning tasks essential for translational and biomedical discovery. To address this limitation, we created and curated eight comprehensive benchmark datasets comprising over 300,000 verifiable question-and-answer pairs, each targeting critical challenges in drug discovery including target druggability, modality suitability, and drug perturbation effects. Using this resource, we developed the OwkinZero models by post-training open-source LLMs through a Reinforcement Learning from Verifiable Rewards strategy. Our results demonstrate that specialized 8-32B OwkinZero models substantially outperform larger, state-of-the-art commercial LLMs on these biological benchmarks. Remarkably, we uncover evidence of a key aspect of generalization: specialist models trained on a single task consistently outperform their base models on previously unseen tasks. This generalization effect is further amplified in our comprehensive OwkinZero models, which were trained on a mixture of datasets and achieve even broader cross-task improvements. This study represents a significant step toward addressing the biological reasoning blind spot in current LLMs, demonstrating that targeted reinforcement learning on carefully curated data can unlock generalizable performance in specialized models, thereby accelerating AI-driven biological discovery.
- **Summary**: The paper "OwkinZero: Accelerating Biological Discovery with AI" introduces a novel approach to enhancing the biological reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs). The authors address the existing limitations of LLMs in core biological tasks crucial for translational and biomedical discovery by creating and curating eight comprehensive benchmark datasets, comprising over 300,000 question-and-answer pairs targeting challenges in drug discovery like target druggability, modality suitability, and drug perturbation effects. They then develop the OwkinZero models by post-training open-source LLMs using Reinforcement Learning from Verifiable Rewards (RLVR), demonstrating that specialized 8-32B models significantly outperform larger, state-of-the-art commercial LLMs on these biological benchmarks. A key finding is the cross-task generalization capabilities of specialist models, where models trained on a single task outperform base models on unseen tasks. The comprehensive OwkinZero models, trained on a mixture of datasets, amplify this generalization effect, achieving even broader cross-task improvements. The paper emphasizes the significance of targeted reinforcement learning on curated data for unlocking generalizable performance in specialized models, accelerating AI-driven biological discovery.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the creation of a comprehensive benchmark specifically designed to evaluate biological reasoning in LLMs. The application of RLVR to fine-tune LLMs on this dataset is also a relatively novel approach in the biomedical domain. The findings regarding cross-task generalization are insightful and suggest a more efficient path towards building specialized AI systems for biology.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a critical gap in current LLMs – their limited ability to perform deep biological reasoning. By demonstrating substantial performance improvements with relatively small, specialized models, the paper offers a potentially more cost-effective and resource-efficient alternative to relying solely on scaling up model size. The datasets and models will be valuable resources for the research community.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Benchmark Datasets:** The meticulously curated datasets are a significant contribution, providing a standardized and challenging testbed for evaluating biological reasoning. The inclusion of proprietary and newly published data minimizes the risk of pre-training data contamination.
    *   **RLVR Application:** The use of RLVR is well-justified, given the scarcity of annotated reasoning traces in biology.
    *   **Performance Gains:** The demonstrated performance improvements over larger, commercial LLMs are compelling and showcase the effectiveness of the targeted fine-tuning approach.
    *   **Generalization Insights:** The findings related to cross-task generalization are intriguing and have implications for how we build specialized AI models for specific domains.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLM Judges for Reasoning Evaluation:** The evaluation of reasoning quality relies on LLM judges, which introduces a subjective element and raises questions about the reliability of these evaluations. The paper acknowledges these limitations.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** While the paper demonstrates the effectiveness of RLVR, more detailed ablation studies exploring the impact of different reward functions, architectures, and training strategies would strengthen the findings.
    *   **Catastrophic Forgetting:** The observation of catastrophic forgetting in some models highlights a challenge for future work, which needs to be addressed to fully realize the potential of specialized LLMs for biological discovery.
    *   **Limited Scope:** The focus on specific tasks in drug discovery means the results may not be generalizable to all areas of biological research.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of AI-driven tools for biological discovery by shifting the focus towards targeted fine-tuning and specialized models. The benchmark datasets and models will serve as valuable resources for the community. The insights into cross-task generalization could also lead to more efficient and effective model development strategies.

Score: 7.5. While the paper presents significant and novel contributions, particularly the benchmark datasets and findings on targeted fine-tuning and generalization, it is not without weaknesses. The reliance on LLM-based reasoning evaluation and the identified catastrophic forgetting effect warrant further investigation. Despite these limitations, the work is a solid step forward and has the potential to accelerate progress in AI-driven biological discovery.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### SATORI: Static Test Oracle Generation for REST APIs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16318v1)
- **Authors**: Juan C. Alonso, Alberto Martin-Lopez, Sergio Segura, Gabriele Bavota, Antonio Ruiz-Cortés
- **Abstract**: REST API test case generation tools are evolving rapidly, with growing capabilities for the automated generation of complex tests. However, despite their strengths in test data generation, these tools are constrained by the types of test oracles they support, often limited to crashes, regressions, and noncompliance with API specifications or design standards. This paper introduces SATORI (Static API Test ORacle Inference), a black-box approach for generating test oracles for REST APIs by analyzing their OpenAPI Specification. SATORI uses large language models to infer the expected behavior of an API by analyzing the properties of the response fields of its operations, such as their name and descriptions. To foster its adoption, we extended the PostmanAssertify tool to automatically convert the test oracles reported by SATORI into executable assertions. Evaluation results on 17 operations from 12 industrial APIs show that SATORI can automatically generate up to hundreds of valid test oracles per operation. SATORI achieved an F1-score of 74.3%, outperforming the state-of-the-art dynamic approach AGORA+ (69.3%)-which requires executing the API-when generating comparable oracle types. Moreover, our findings show that static and dynamic oracle inference methods are complementary: together, SATORI and AGORA+ found 90% of the oracles in our annotated ground-truth dataset. Notably, SATORI uncovered 18 bugs in popular APIs (Amadeus Hotel, Deutschebahn, FDIC, GitLab, Marvel, OMDb and Vimeo) leading to documentation updates by the API maintainers.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SATORI: Static Test Oracle Generation for REST APIs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SATORI, a static, black-box approach to automatically generating test oracles for REST APIs by analyzing their OpenAPI Specification (OAS). SATORI leverages large language models (LLMs) to infer the expected behavior of an API by analyzing the properties of response fields. The authors extended the PostmanAssertify tool to convert SATORI's output into executable assertions. The evaluation on 17 operations from 12 industrial APIs demonstrates that SATORI can generate hundreds of valid test oracles per operation with competitive F1-score (74.3%) when compared to the dynamic approach AGORA+. Notably, SATORI uncovered 18 bugs in popular APIs, leading to documentation updates. SATORI does not require prior API executions, offering a cost-effective solution compared to dynamic approaches. The authors provide a dataset, OKAMI, and an extension of PostmanAssertify to foster adoption and benchmarking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the *static* generation of test oracles using LLMs, contrasting with existing *dynamic* approaches like AGORA+ that rely on API execution to infer invariants. While LLMs have been used in software testing before, their application to *static* oracle generation for *REST APIs* represents a worthwhile advance. The idea of extracting information directly from the OAS is clever, and provides a cost-effective solution. However, it's still reliant on the quality and completeness of the OAS.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in REST API testing: the lack of robust and automated test oracles beyond basic specification conformance and crash detection. The ability to generate domain-specific oracles from the OAS has significant practical implications. The discovery of real bugs in prominent APIs underscores its potential impact. The OKAMI dataset is a significant contribution, facilitating further research.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear problem statement and motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches and the need for more effective test oracles.
    *   **Well-defined approach:** SATORI's architecture and the use of LLMs for static analysis are well explained.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The evaluation is thorough, covering multiple LLMs, comparing against a state-of-the-art dynamic approach, and assessing fault detection capabilities.
    *   **Practical contributions:** The release of the OKAMI dataset, the PostmanAssertify extension, and the documentation of discovered bugs significantly enhance the paper's impact.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on OAS Quality:** SATORI's reliance on OAS quality is both a strength and a weakness. Incomplete or inaccurate OAS documents can limit the effectiveness of generated oracles. The paper could explore the impact of low-quality OAS specifications more thoroughly. While the paper mentions updating specifications, it doesn't address how the performance would be affected by an initial poor OAS.
    *   **Limited Oracle Types:**  The focus on unary invariants simplifies the evaluation, but limits the scope of the generated oracles. Future work should extend SATORI to support n-ary invariants and complex dependencies.
    *   **LLM Choice Rigor:** While 21 LLMs were initially evaluated, the study doesn't offer a great deal of insight into the specific capabilities of LLMs that make them good or bad at static oracle generation. The link between *why* GPT-4o is superior isn't clear, only *that* it is superior. This limits the generalizability, especially as the field of LLMs is rapidly evolving.
    *   **Threats to Validity:** The threat to validity is adequately addressed in the paper, but the extent to which the test cases generated depend on the structure of the OAS, and not its actual function, is a concern.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of REST API testing by promoting static oracle generation techniques and providing a valuable resource for benchmarking and comparing different approaches.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of 8. The paper presents a valuable and well-executed approach to static test oracle generation for REST APIs. The use of LLMs to analyze OAS specifications and infer domain-specific test oracles is innovative and offers a cost-effective alternative to dynamic approaches. The thorough evaluation, practical contributions, and the OKAMI dataset enhance the paper's significance. While the reliance on OAS quality, the limitation of unary invariants, and the absence of a thorough LLM selection strategy are shortcomings, they do not diminish the paper's overall contribution. The discovery of real-world bugs further solidifies the method's importance. The study's comprehensive nature and its provision of valuable resources warrant the high score, even if there are areas for further research and improvement.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLMSymGuard: A Symbolic Safety Guardrail Framework Leveraging Interpretable Jailbreak Concepts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16325v1)
- **Authors**: Darpan Aswal, Céline Hudelot
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models have found success in a variety of applications; however, their safety remains a matter of concern due to the existence of various types of jailbreaking methods. Despite significant efforts, alignment and safety fine-tuning only provide a certain degree of robustness against jailbreak attacks that covertly mislead LLMs towards the generation of harmful content. This leaves them prone to a number of vulnerabilities, ranging from targeted misuse to accidental profiling of users. This work introduces \textbf{LLMSymGuard}, a novel framework that leverages Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) to identify interpretable concepts within LLM internals associated with different jailbreak themes. By extracting semantically meaningful internal representations, LLMSymGuard enables building symbolic, logical safety guardrails -- offering transparent and robust defenses without sacrificing model capabilities or requiring further fine-tuning. Leveraging advances in mechanistic interpretability of LLMs, our approach demonstrates that LLMs learn human-interpretable concepts from jailbreaks, and provides a foundation for designing more interpretable and logical safeguard measures against attackers. Code will be released upon publication.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLMSymGuard, a framework for building symbolic safety guardrails for Large Language Models (LLMs) that leverages Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) to extract interpretable concepts associated with jailbreak themes from LLM internal representations. By identifying these semantically meaningful internal representations, LLMSymGuard constructs symbolic, logical safety guardrails that offer transparent and robust defenses *without* requiring further fine-tuning or sacrificing model capabilities. The framework first trains SAEs on LLM activations from jailbreak prompts, classifies features (identifying "dead," "junk," and "rich" features), categorizes the "rich" features based on jailbreak themes, and then designs symbolic guardrails that block unsafe prompts based on logical conditions applied to the activations of these rich features. The authors show that these guardrails can effectively block jailbreak attempts while preserving model capabilities.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The idea of using mechanistic interpretability (MI) techniques, specifically SAEs, to understand and mitigate jailbreaking attacks on LLMs has a moderate level of novelty. Prior work has explored using SAEs for detoxification and prompt feature characterization. However, the *specific* application of identifying interpretable *latent* SAE features to construct a *fully symbolic* guardrail framework *without fine-tuning* appears to be a relatively fresh approach. The key distinguishing factor is the combination of SAE-based feature extraction with the creation of interpretable *logical* filtering mechanisms rather than directly manipulating the model weights or performing further training.

**Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in its potential to address the limitations of current safety measures based on fine-tuning and RLHF.  These techniques often trade off safety for model capabilities and lack transparency.  LLMSymGuard offers a more interpretable and potentially more robust defense against jailbreaks. By grounding the guardrails in human-understandable concepts, the framework allows for better understanding and control over the safety mechanisms.  The claim of preserving model capabilities is also significant, as it suggests a way to improve safety without sacrificing performance on other tasks. The approach allows for greater visibility into how the guardrail works, which is an improvement over black-box fine-tuning based methods.

**Strengths:**

*   **Interpretable Approach:** A core strength is the emphasis on interpretability. The framework is designed to extract human-understandable concepts, enabling better analysis and modification of safety rules.
*   **No Fine-Tuning Required:** The claim of not requiring additional fine-tuning is a significant advantage. It avoids the trade-offs and challenges associated with fine-tuning-based methods.
*   **Clear Methodology:**  The paper presents a well-defined and clearly described methodology for building symbolic guardrails.
*   **Evaluation:**  The experimental setup and evaluation metrics are appropriate for assessing the performance of the guardrail.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is limited to a single, relatively small (1B parameter) LLM (Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct) and a specific hook point in the first transformer block. The generalizability of the results to larger models or different architectures is unclear. A more comprehensive evaluation across various models and layers would strengthen the findings.
*   **Data Dependency:** The effectiveness of the guardrail heavily relies on the quality and diversity of the jailbreak prompts used for SAE training. Bias in the training data can lead to skewed feature extraction and ineffective guardrails.
*   **Feature Polysemanticity:** While the paper acknowledges the polysemantic nature of SAE features, this remains a key challenge.  The difficulty in assigning unambiguous interpretations to the features may complicate the design of precise and reliable safety rules. The persistent polysemanticity of these features remains a key challenge, complicating precise attribution and the formulation of unambiguous safety rules.
*   **Complexity:**  The framework involves several steps (SAE training, feature classification, rule design), which might make it complex to implement and maintain in practice. A simpler, more streamlined approach could enhance its usability.
*   **Guardrail Rule Design:** The specific guardrail rules evaluated in the paper are relatively simple. More sophisticated logical rules might be needed to achieve optimal performance in real-world scenarios. Also, some of these rules have either insufficient safety from harm (All-AND) or are impractical with insufficient safety for general deployment.
*   **Ethical Considerations:** The ethical considerations section should be expanded to include the risk of biased filtering creating trade-offs with over-censorship.

**Overall:**

LLMSymGuard presents a promising approach to building more interpretable and robust safety guardrails for LLMs. However, the experimental evaluation is currently limited, and the challenges associated with feature polysemanticity and data dependency need to be addressed further. The paper needs a more extensive evaluation to properly validate the claims about the framework's generalizability and effectiveness.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:** The paper demonstrates a moderately novel approach with significant potential to improve LLM safety. However, the limited scope of the experimental evaluation and the challenges associated with feature polysemanticity prevent a higher score. If the authors were to address these limitations in future work, the framework could become a highly valuable tool for building trustworthy and reliable LLM-based applications.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### From Linear to Hierarchical: Evolving Tree-structured Thoughts for Efficient Alpha Mining
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16334v1)
- **Authors**: Junji Ren, Junjie Zhao, Shengcai Liu, Peng Yang
- **Abstract**: Alpha mining, which discovers signals that predict asset returns, has long been attractive for automatic quantitative investment. This problem is typically formulated as a tree-based symbolic regression with handcrafted market data features and arithmetic operators. Unfortunately, existing symbolic methods are concerned with computational inefficiency and dependence on prior knowledge. Recent implementation of Large Language Models (LLMs) show that they can automatically generate executable codes for various tasks efficiently, thus can be considered as a new promising way for alpha mining. Specifically, LLMs-driven methods evolve a set of heuristics, including thoughts and codes, where the thoughts are usually represented as plain-text prompts of codes. Unfortunately, trivially adopting them in alpha mining ignores the fact that alphas are with hierarchical tree structures. This paper introduces Tree-structured thought Evolution (TreEvo), which evolves hierarchical reasoning ideas solely at the thought level. Experiments on four real-market datasets demonstrate that TreEvo can obtain better alphas with much less computational time and human expert efforts. And this superiority hardly holds without the tree-structured thoughts and the compatible evolutionary operators.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "From Linear to Hierarchical: Evolving Tree-structured Thoughts for Efficient Alpha Mining" introduces a novel approach called Tree-structured thought Evolution (TreEvo) for alpha mining. Unlike existing methods that use Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate code or plain-text thoughts alongside code, TreEvo evolves hierarchical tree-structured thoughts. These trees represent the reasoning process behind alpha generation, with sub-trees encapsulating sub-components of reasoning and higher-level nodes representing more abstract heuristics.  The authors design semantic-level evolutionary operators (crossover, mutation, pruning) tailored for these tree structures. The experiments demonstrate that TreEvo achieves better alpha mining performance (in terms of Information Coefficient (IC) and computational efficiency) compared to traditional symbolic methods and other LLM-driven approaches on several real-market datasets.  The authors also conduct ablation studies to show the importance of both the tree-structured representation and the novel evolutionary operators.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of evolving tree-structured thoughts, rather than just code or plain-text thoughts alongside code, is a significant contribution.  It brings back the concept of explicit hierarchical structure common in symbolic regression but in a way that leverages the expressiveness of LLMs. The design of semantic-level evolutionary operators specifically for tree-structured thoughts is also novel. The approach directly addresses limitations of existing LLM-driven EAs and offers a way to better organize and manipulate reasoning ideas.

* **Significance:** Alpha mining is a crucial task in quantitative finance.  The paper's results demonstrate that TreEvo can discover more predictive alpha signals with less computational effort than traditional approaches and other LLM-driven EAs. This has practical implications for the efficiency and effectiveness of automated trading strategies. The improved interpretability stemming from the tree structure can also make strategies more reliable and easier to validate.  The paper pushes the boundaries of how LLMs can be used for symbolic regression-like tasks and demonstrates a valuable use case.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear problem definition and motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches and motivates the need for a hierarchical approach.
    * **Well-designed method:** TreEvo is well-designed and combines LLMs with evolutionary algorithms in a meaningful way. The semantic-level operators are well-motivated by the structure of the thoughts.
    * **Thorough experiments:** The experiments are comprehensive, covering different datasets and comparing TreEvo against several relevant baselines. The ablation studies effectively isolate the contributions of the tree structure and evolutionary operators. The use of IC and Rank IC provides a robust evaluation.
    * **Quantified improvements:** The improvements in IC are not only statistically significant but also practically meaningful in the context of alpha mining. The computational efficiency gains are also substantial.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited LLM Interaction Details:** While the prompts used for thought initialization and code generation are provided, it would be beneficial to have a more detailed explanation of how the LLM interacts with the tree structures during the evolutionary process. For example, how are prompts generated for subtree mutation or crossover?  How is coherence maintained?
    * **Generality of Operators:** Although the experiments are convincing, the performance of the evolutionary operators might be highly dependent on the specific prompt engineering and the pre-training of the particular LLM used (Qwen3-Max). Are these operators easily adaptable to other LLMs or different problem domains? The paper does not adequately address the generality of the proposed approach.
    * **Explainability/Interpretability Depth:** While the tree-structure offers better organization, it would be valuable to see some examples of the resulting thought trees and a detailed discussion of how they improve interpretability *in practice*. How easy is it to understand *why* a specific alpha factor is generated?  Are the intermediate reasoning steps truly understandable? The paper highlights improved interpretability but doesn't fully demonstrate it with concrete examples. The emphasis seems to be on performance improvements, rather than deep explainability.
    * **Scalability:** The paper fixes the population size to 10. An analysis of how TreEvo scales with increasing population size would be useful.

* **Score Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially significant contribution to the field of alpha mining and LLM-driven symbolic regression. The idea of evolving tree-structured thoughts addresses limitations of existing approaches and enables more efficient and effective alpha discovery. The empirical results convincingly demonstrate the benefits of TreEvo. However, the paper could be strengthened by providing more details on the LLM interaction, addressing the generality of the operators, and providing a deeper analysis of the interpretability of the resulting thought trees. The limited scalability information is also a minor concern. The improvements are significant, but the discussion about limitations and scalability is lacking.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Confusion is the Final Barrier: Rethinking Jailbreak Evaluation and Investigating the Real Misuse Threat of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16347v1)
- **Authors**: Yu Yan, Sheng Sun, Zhe Wang, Yijun Lin, Zenghao Duan, zhifei zheng, Min Liu, Zhiyi yin, Jianping Zhang
- **Abstract**: With the development of Large Language Models (LLMs), numerous efforts have revealed their vulnerabilities to jailbreak attacks. Although these studies have driven the progress in LLMs' safety alignment, it remains unclear whether LLMs have internalized authentic knowledge to deal with real-world crimes, or are merely forced to simulate toxic language patterns. This ambiguity raises concerns that jailbreak success is often attributable to a hallucination loop between jailbroken LLM and judger LLM. By decoupling the use of jailbreak techniques, we construct knowledge-intensive Q\&A to investigate the misuse threats of LLMs in terms of dangerous knowledge possession, harmful task planning utility, and harmfulness judgment robustness. Experiments reveal a mismatch between jailbreak success rates and harmful knowledge possession in LLMs, and existing LLM-as-a-judge frameworks tend to anchor harmfulness judgments on toxic language patterns. Our study reveals a gap between existing LLM safety assessments and real-world threat potential.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, focusing on its novelty and significance:

**Summary:**

The paper "Confusion is the Final Barrier: Rethinking Jailbreak Evaluation and Investigating the Real Misuse Threat of LLMs" argues that current jailbreak evaluations for Large Language Models (LLMs) are often misleading. They attribute jailbreak success primarily to a hallucination loop between the jailbroken LLM and the LLM used as a judge, rather than a genuine understanding of harmful knowledge.  The authors introduce VENOM, a framework that decouples jailbreak attacks from the evaluation of LLMs' *authentic* criminal capacities. VENOM assesses dangerous knowledge possession, harmful task planning utility, and harmfulness judgment robustness through knowledge-intensive Q&A and counterfactual task design grounded in real-world criminal sources. Their experiments reveal a mismatch between jailbreak success rates and actual harmful knowledge possession in LLMs. They also demonstrate that existing LLM-as-a-judge frameworks are often biased towards toxic language patterns and fail to identify truly threatening content.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Problem Framing:** The paper identifies a crucial weakness in the existing LLM security landscape.  It challenges the assumption that successful jailbreak necessarily implies a real-world misuse threat. This is a significant contribution because it shifts the focus from simply bypassing safety measures to understanding the *depth* and *actionability* of harmful knowledge within LLMs.
*   **Robust Methodology:**  The introduction of the VENOM framework is a key strength. Decoupling the jailbreak process allows for a more direct and controlled assessment of LLM capabilities.  The use of real-world criminal knowledge (sourced from dark web forums and expert manuals) for evaluation is a commendable effort to ensure relevance and realism. The counterfactual task design is clever and allows for the assessment of planning abilities independently from the presence of harmful intent, a crucial decoupling.
*   **Empirical Validation:**  The experimental results are compelling. The mismatch between jailbreak success and actual dangerous knowledge possession clearly demonstrates the limitations of current evaluation methods. The analysis of the LLM-as-a-judge frameworks highlights their susceptibility to surface-level toxicity and their inability to reliably identify fact-based threats, which is a serious issue.
*   **Practical Implications:** The paper's findings have important practical implications. It suggests that security efforts should focus not only on preventing jailbreaks but also on limiting the acquisition and coherent organization of dangerous knowledge within LLMs. The paper motivates further research into more robust and reliable safety evaluation frameworks.
*   **Well-written and Clear:** The paper is clearly written and well-structured, making the complex ideas accessible.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Domain Limitation:** The paper focuses on a relatively limited set of criminal domains (drug synthesis, explosives/firearms, and financial fraud).  While these are important, they do not encompass the full spectrum of potential LLM misuse. The authors acknowledge this limitation.  It is not clear how well the VENOM framework generalizes to areas such as cybercrime or misinformation campaigns.
*   **Subjectivity in Knowledge and Counterfactual Task Curation:**  While the authors aim for objectivity, the selection of knowledge and design of counterfactual tasks inevitably involve some degree of subjectivity.  Different researchers might choose different sources or design different tasks, potentially affecting the results.  However, the use of real-world materials and model-based crowdsourcing to generate metaphors mitigate the level of possible subjectivity.
